\displaystyle\geq\left(\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}N(l)\frac{\tau_{l}^{4\beta+2}}{(\tau_{l}^{2}+\eta)^{2}}\right)^{2}=\VERT(\mathscr{K}+\eta I)^{-2}\mathscr{K}^{2\beta+1}\VERT_{1}^{2}.

(S.38)

Then applying
\beta_{n},\eta_{n}
, instead of
\beta
, to (
C.5
) demonstrates that
\eta_{n}
satisfies the conditions for not only
Theorem

C.6
but also
Theorem

C.5
, thus the claim holds. Moreover, we also get
c_{n}^{2}=O(c^{\otimes}_{n})
and
a_{n}^{2}=O(a^{\otimes}_{n})
.

Observe using the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}+\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}},

which leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}^{2}>D\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}^{2}>D\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}^{2}>D\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}^{2}>D\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}>D\max(c_{n},a_{n},n^{-1})\right].

Finally,

\displaystyle\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}\otimes\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu}\otimes\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathscr{K}^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}+2\|\mathscr{K}^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}}\|\mathscr{K}^{\beta}\boldsymbol{\mu}\|_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\|\mathscr{K}^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\mu})\|_{\mathbb{H}})

and the claim in the beginning of the proof yields the result.
∎

Again,
Theorem

C.6
and
Corollary

C.7
reveal the trade-off of the convergence rate concerning the decaying speed of the penalty parameter
\eta_{n}>0
. When
\eta_{n}
decreases faster,
c_{n}^{\otimes}
decreases faster but
a_{n}^{\otimes}
decreases more slowly. Specifically, when
\tau_{l}\asymp l^{-p}
and
N(l)\asymp l^{q}
for some
p,q>0
with
2p>q+1
, it holds for any
\beta\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\VERT(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes}+\eta I)^{-2}(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{2\beta+1}\VERT_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}N(l)N(l^{\prime})\frac{\tau_{ll^{\prime}}^{4\beta+2}}{(\tau_{ll^{\prime}}^{2}+\eta)^{2}}=\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}\frac{N(l)N(l^{\prime})\tau_{ll^{\prime}}^{-2(1-2\beta)}}{(1+\eta\tau_{ll^{\prime}}^{-2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\asymp\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(l^{2p}l^{\prime 2p})^{\frac{q+2p(1-2\beta)}{2p}}(1+\eta l^{2p}l^{\prime 2p})^{-2}\asymp\eta^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-2\beta)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta}\right),

(S.39)

where we used Lemma 2.3 in
[
58
]
. Using (
C.5
), one may show as in
[
9
]
that

a_{n}^{\otimes}=O(\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1}))\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\eta_{n}^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-2\beta)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta_{n}}\right)=O\left(\max\left[\overline{r},(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}}))\eta_{n}^{2(\beta+\kappa_{2})}\right]\right),

(S.40)

is satisfied regardless of the value of
\beta\geq 0
if we choose

\eta_{n}\asymp\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}+2\kappa_{2})^{-1}}.

Proof of
Theorem

7.6
.

Take
\beta=1/2
, which leads to

\|(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{\beta}(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}^{2}=\|(\mathscr{P}\otimes\mathscr{P})(\hat{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}_{\eta_{n}}-\boldsymbol{\Gamma})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathbb{G}\times\Omega^{d-d_{r}},W_{d_{r},d})}^{2},

by (
15
). Also, choose
\kappa_{2}=0
and
\eta_{n}\asymp(n\overline{r}/\log n)^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p})^{-1}}
so that

\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\asymp\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})=\max(\eta_{n},n^{-1}),

in view of (
S.40
). It remains to check whether the assumptions other than (A) in
Theorem

C.6
, or equivalently
Corollary

C.7
, hold. First, we verify

\displaystyle\VERT(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes}+\eta_{n}I)^{-2}(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{2\beta+1}\VERT_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tr:norm:rate:cov}}}{{\asymp}}\eta_{n}^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-1)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)^{(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-1)(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p})^{-1}}\log\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})\right)^{(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-1)(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p})^{-1}}\log\left(n\overline{r}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=o(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})).

Second, choose
\theta\in(\frac{q+1}{2p},1)\neq\emptyset
and set
\zeta:=(1-\theta)/2\in(0,1/2]
. Then,
-2\theta p+q<-1
so
\VERT\mathscr{K}^{1-2\zeta}\VERT_{1}<\infty
, and

\displaystyle\VERT(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes}+\eta I)^{-2}(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{2\zeta+1}\VERT_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tr:norm:rate:cov}}}{{\asymp}}\eta_{n}^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-2\zeta)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)^{(\frac{q+1}{2p}+\theta)(\frac{q+1}{2p}+1)^{-1}}\log\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=o(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})).

∎

Proof of
Theorem

7.7
.

Take
\beta=0
,
\kappa_{2}>0
, and choose
\eta_{n}\asymp(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})/\log n)^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}+2\kappa_{2})^{-1}}
so that

\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},a_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})\asymp\max(c_{n}^{\otimes},n^{-1})=\max(\eta_{n}^{2\kappa_{2}},n^{-1}),

in view of (
S.40
). It remains to check whether the assumptions other than (A) in
Theorem

C.6
, or equivalently
Corollary

C.7
, hold. First, we verify

\displaystyle\VERT(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes}+\eta_{n}I)^{-2}(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{2\beta+1}\VERT_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tr:norm:rate:cov}}}{{\asymp}}\eta_{n}^{-\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)^{\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}+2\kappa_{2})^{-1}}\log\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})\right)^{\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}+2\kappa_{2})^{-1}}\log\left(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=o(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})).

Second, choose
\theta\in(\frac{q+1}{2p},1)\neq\emptyset
and set
\zeta:=(1-\theta)/2\in(0,1/2]
. Then,
-2\theta p+q<-1
so
\VERT\mathscr{K}^{1-2\zeta}\VERT_{1}<\infty
, and

\displaystyle\VERT(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes}+\eta_{n}I)^{-2}(\mathscr{K}^{\otimes})^{2\zeta+1}\VERT_{1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:tr:norm:rate:cov}}}{{\asymp}}\eta_{n}^{-(\frac{2p+q+1}{2p}-2\zeta)}\log\left(\frac{1}{\eta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)^{(\frac{q+1}{2p}+1+2\kappa_{1})^{-1}(\frac{q+1}{2p}+\theta)}\log\left(\frac{n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})}{\log n}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})\right)^{(\frac{q+1}{2p}+1+2\kappa_{1})^{-1}(\frac{q+1}{2p}+\theta)}\log\left(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=o(n\cdot\min(\overline{r},\overline{r}^{\sigma^{4}})).

∎

C.6
Proofs in Section
8

Proof of
Proposition

8.2
.

Using the tower property, the conditional expectation is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{ijk}|(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}_{i}}[Z_{ijk}|(\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})]=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}_{i}}[\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\varphi(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij}\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}(I,\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

For
i\neq i^{\prime}
, the conditional second moment is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{ijk}Z_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}|(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk}),(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{X}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[Z_{ijk}|(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})]\mathbb{E}[Z_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}|(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{X}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}})]

\displaystyle=\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}k^{\prime}}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}.

On the other hand, when
i=i^{\prime}
, using (
9
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Z_{ijk}Z_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}|(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk}),(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{X}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}_{i}}[Z_{ijk}Z_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}}|(\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk}),(\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{X}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}_{i}}[\langle\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\varphi(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij}\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\otimes\varphi(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}]+\sigma_{r_{i}}^{2}\delta_{jj^{\prime}}\delta_{kk^{\prime}}.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}_{i}}[\varphi(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij}\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})\otimes\varphi(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{R}_{i},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk})]=\overline{\varphi\otimes\varphi}(\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij},\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk},\tilde{\boldsymbol{R}}_{ij^{\prime}},\boldsymbol{X}_{ij^{\prime}k^{\prime}})=\overline{\varphi}_{iJJ^{\prime}}^{\otimes},

which yields (
8.2
). Finally, due to the isotropy of
\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}
, it holds that
\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}=\rho(R)\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}
for any
R\in\mathbb{G}
, hence

\displaystyle\langle\overline{\boldsymbol{\mu}},\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}}[\langle\rho(\boldsymbol{R})\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}}[\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\rho(\boldsymbol{R}^{-1})\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}]=\langle\boldsymbol{\mu},\overline{\varphi}_{ijk}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}},

using the invariance of the Haar measure. Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\overline{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}},\overline{\varphi}_{iJJ^{\prime}}^{\otimes}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}}[\langle\rho(\boldsymbol{R})\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\rho(\boldsymbol{R}^{-1}),\overline{\varphi}_{iJJ^{\prime}}^{\otimes}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{R}}[\langle\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\rho(\boldsymbol{R}^{-1})\overline{\varphi}_{iJJ^{\prime}}^{\otimes}\rho(\boldsymbol{R})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}]=\langle\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\overline{\varphi}_{iJJ^{\prime}}^{\otimes}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}\otimes\mathbb{H}}.

∎

D
Index

•

\Omega^{d},\Omega^{d-d_{r}}
: Compact domains of input and output functions.

•

W_{0,d},W_{d_{r},d}
: Weight functions for input and output spaces.

•

w_{0,d},w_{d_{r},d}
: Total weights (normalization constants).

•

\mathscr{P}
: Sinogram or forward operator.

•

\mathbb{G}
: Compact Lie group representing orientations.

•

\rho
: Unitary group representation acting on
\mathbb{G}
.

•

K
: Mercer kernel on the input space.

•

\tilde{K}
: Induced kernel on the output (observation) space.

•

\varphi
: Kernel feature map.

•

\mathcal{Y}_{i}
: Random function in the RKHS
\mathbb{H}(K)
.

•

\boldsymbol{\mu},\boldsymbol{\Gamma},\boldsymbol{\Sigma}
: Mean, second-moment, and covariance functions.

•

\boldsymbol{R}_{ij}
: Orientation (viewing direction) for the
j
-th observation of
i
-th function.

•

\boldsymbol{X}_{ijk}
: Spatial location (e.g., detector position) for sample
(i,j,k)
.

•

\varepsilon_{ijk}
: Additive noise at observation
(i,j,k)
with heteroscadestic variance
\sigma_{r_{i}}^{2}
.

•

Z_{ijk}
: Observed scalar value at sample
(i,j,k)
.

•

\boldsymbol{\Phi},\boldsymbol{\Phi}^{\odot}
: Mean and covariance Gram matrices.

•

\nu,\eta
: Tikhonov regularization parameters for mean and second-moment estimation.

•

\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\nu},\hat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\eta}^{\odot}
: Coefficient vectors for the representer theorem solutions.

•

\mathscr{T}_{K}
: Integral operator associated with kernel
K
.

•

\imath
: Continuous embedding map of RKHS into
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

•

g_{lm},e_{lm},\tau_{l}^{2}
: Eigenfunctions and eigenvalues of
\mathscr{T}_{\tilde{K}}
and
\mathscr{K}
.

•

p,q
: Exponents governing eigenvalue decay and multiplicity growth.

•

\kappa,\delta,\beta,\zeta
: Source condition parameters for convergence analysis.

•

\mathcal{S}
: Class of probability measures satisfying source conditions.

•

\gamma
: Bandwidth parameter of the Gaussian kernel.

•

\mathcal{J},\mathscr{J}
: Off-diagonal index set and associated elimination tensor.

•

i,j,k,l,m
: Index variables for function, orientation, location, eigenvalue, and multiplicity.

•

n,r,s,\mathbb{N},N(l)
: Cardinalities for indices
i,j,k,l,m
.

•

G
: Green’s function.

•

C_{l}^{\lambda}
: Gegenbauer polynomials.

•

\Gamma
: Gamma function.

•

\delta_{jj^{\prime}}
: Kronecker delta.
